ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00086_d7e3552	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00086_d7e3567	That's where I can take a short look. The main changes concern the right to share. We have removed the right of duty for the ancestors, we have strengthened the right of inheritance for the spouses in connection with this, and we have, in particular, provided the possibility for the heirs to be able to pay out dues with corresponding partial payments, with grants, in any case also under judicial control, so that economic units do not necessarily have to be destroyed. I think this is the whole central point: the possibility of not having to satisfy part-time claims immediately, but in the interests of maintaining economic units, small businesses, family businesses and the like, to have a longer period for them. In addition, there is a novum that will be really visible to the individual citizen: the possibility that care services provided by relatives should be taken into account on their own in the course of the leave process, provided that they have not already been paid. I also think that is very important, and it was also a very important concern for us to find a regulation for these services, which are often provided in the relationship environment, and in which it is often the case that especially those who produce them, because no one else is willing to do so, are not necessarily those who are then consistently aware of their demands. That is what we want to change, and in the future it will be so that, in the course of a leave process, the Commissioner of the Court will find a corresponding compensation, and I think that will in many cases also succeed. It is also very important to me that this has been found in the new inheritance law.

It would have been a chance to modernise Greece's membership of the European Union, in the euro. Only this opportunity has not been taken by the Greek governments, but they have used the low interest rates the euro has brought to debt. First of all the public sector, then the private sector has followed, then wages have increased, first in the public sector, then the private sector has to take part. That would be Not in itself the problem when wages rise. The problem is only if productivity does not rise, and that has happened. As a result, this Greek financial and economic policy has led to the country that is no longer competitive in the Greek economy. And you need to know that when you're looking for solutions. And how do we stand now? What is the situation now, seven years after the financial crisis, after five years of rescue programmes? Some €300 billion went to Greece, in one form or another. Yes, some of it to banks. That is also to be criticized. It would have been better if banks had been saved directly – and not over the Greek detour. But it's not all flown in there, about a third of it has flown to banks. The rest has been used to keep and finance a system that chronically spends more than it takes over water. What has definitely not happened is reforms, even that is clear, necessary structural reforms. What has already happened: that in these five years rescue programmes the creditor structure has completely changed. According to a majority of private creditors, we have come to an almost exclusively public creditor structure. Now, the European taxpayers are paying. The tragic thing about this is that the dispute is now being fought not between Greece and private banks and investors, but between Greece and other European states. In other words, this restructuring of Greek debt from private to public has damaged the European integration project over the last five years. This was certainly a mistake that we, too, must face up to the European states. But how do we get out of this mess now? – We certainly do not get out of this mess if we continue the course of the last five years. Because, as it's so beautiful: Insanity is when you do the same again and again and believe that something else comes out! Therefore, we cannot continue to do the same and believe that the situation is changing in Greece now. At the expense of the European and Austrian taxpayers, we cannot continue to pour billions into a barrel without soil if it remains a barrel without soil. And a barrel without soil remains if no structural reforms occur. Now it is also necessary to maintain clearly and clearly that reforms are not enforceable from the outside. This doesn't work for individual people, it doesn't work for countries either. You can only change yourself if you are internally convinced that change is necessary, and then you follow this path. That is to say, the solution is not in Brussels, but the solution lies in Athens. The solution lies with the Greek government. This Greek government must take responsibility! It does not matter whether it assumes this responsibility by introducing reforms within or outside the euro – that has all advantages and disadvantages – but it is crucial that the Greek government assumes responsibility, decides on a path and continues to pursue this path consistently. We need this leadership responsibility in this common European House. If we do not have them, this common European house will continue to be damaged. If the Greek Government takes responsibility for leadership, it will not lack European solidarity either. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00086_d7e12971	Mr President. Minister! High house! We all know the reasons why this novelle of the Federal Theatre Organisation Act has become necessary. On the one hand, the reason for this was that there was a grosser financial scandal at Burgtheater, whose exact backgrounds still concern the courts and where we do not know exactly how far criminal activities have taken place, but at least this is very likely, on the other hand, we know that the Court of Auditors has revealed several fundamental problems in federal theatre holding in a report a year ago. I just mention some: for example, that the strategic management in the holding did not work, that, for example, multi-annual plans were not realizable or realistic, that gross funding holes appeared, that, for example, coordination between the ministry and the holding did not work, for example, that contract extensions were made without actually making calls – for example, the managing director Springer, but also for the individual houses. So all these are things that have shown the need for action. We liberals were – and this is known – from the beginning for reintegration of the holding into the federal government, and above all because of that – which has proved to be true in the last two years – because we have not been able to exercise any or no parliamentary control. We must not forget that the Bundestheater is receiving some € 148 million from the federal government, from the public sector, by the way, with this innovation, we think it is wrong to increase to € 162 million – much, much tax money, for which good and careful control by Parliament would definitely be necessary, but which was not really possible in the past. For us, then, this reintegration would have been a way. In our view, an alternative that has been shown would have been a real strengthening of holding while at the same time introducing better parliamentary control rights. – However, none of them, neither the inclusion nor the actual strengthening of the holding, will be implemented with this novel. I already mentioned it: The basic fee will be increased by € 14 million. This is not justified in our view, because we have seen, thanks to various evaluations, that there would be great savings potential and we could save the taxpayer a lot of money. Moreover, there is now no real strengthening of holding, only a supposed, while at the same time strengthening the Minister, but without appropriate control rights. – All of this is to be rejected from our point of view, which is why we will vote against this novel. In addition, there is a small part of it, about which we also wonder, namely that in the novelle of the state operal ball is entered in the cultural policy mission, on the grounds that one then wants to save one's taxes in the execution of this quite successful ball. In my opinion, it is, or we believe, a completely false signal in times when we introduce the registration obligation to increase VAT rates in the hotel and cultural business, that the State, a public institution, is, so to speak, saving taxes. ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00086_d7e13019	Mrs Hakel. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00086_d7e13041	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Over the past one and a half years, I am convinced that we have not dealt with any cultural institution as comprehensively and intensively as with the federal theatres and, of course, with the castle theatre and the financial scandal there. Today's novel of the Federal Theatre Organisation Act lays down the framework conditions for the Bundestheater both organisationally and financially, and that is, the rules now provided for lay down clear responsibilities. Let me explain briefly what that means. The individual daughters of the Bundestheaterholding, i.e. the Staatsoper, the Burgtheater and the Volksoper, remain independent in their artistic responsibility, and that is a central, important point for me. The Federal Minister for Art and Culture will also, as before, be responsible for the cultural policy guidelines or framework conditions. In the future, the Bundestheaterholding will take over even greater economic governance of all subsidiaries, and this means that the future holding director – this position will be announced this year – will be in close contact with the individual subsidiaries. Its task will be to live up to and implement the rules and guidelines laid down by the Ministry. He will control the efficient interaction of the theater and must have a watchful eye on control. In the future, the Minister of Culture will also decide not only artistic management, but also the personnel decisions in the commercial field. This is also seen by the Bundestheater as a clear appreciation of the business manager, and that is also good. Another important point, which will continue to be decided by the Federal Minister for Art and Culture in the future, is the distribution of the basic salary. The basic fee – and this has always become clear in the Court of Auditors’ subcommittee – has not been sufficiently adjusted to inflation over the last 15 years and is now clearly too low, despite drastic savings, especially in the personnel sector and the main reason for the financial bottlenecks of the Bundestheaterholding. Therefore, there can only be a logical and above all correct consequence, namely to increase the basic salary from 2016. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] At the same time, a three-year performance and target agreement will create an important new instrument to enable the Federal Theatres to ensure multi-annual planning security. Performance and target agreements will be concluded between the Ministry of Culture and Holding, as well as between holding companies and subsidiaries, including staff, revenue and expenditure plans to ensure balanced budgets. It is also to be welcomed that in the future, the management of each company – which is also an important innovation – must present a business concept within six months of the appointment. This will contribute to greater transparency and will clearly explain the path that the theaters will take in the coming years. I have worked very intensively and in-depthly on the novelle of the Federal Theatre Organisation Act and was also involved in all processes, so today I can say out of full conviction that there is a good novel here, which should prevent similar financial scandals, as they happened at Burgtheater, from happening again. Holding is positioned as a strategic management holding company and optimises its structure and specifies its tasks. Your skills are clearly defined. This implements the largest structural reform of the Bundestheater for 15 years and secures the future of the Bundestheater in the long term. I would also like to express my express thanks to Federal Minister Ostermayer for acting quickly on this issue in the past and for drawing the right conclusions based on facts.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00086_d7e14072	Next speaker: Mr Christ. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00086_d7e14093	Thank you very much, Madam President. Federal Minister! High house! I would just like to explain the art promotion law briefly to the spectators so that they know what it is all about. Among other things, the Kunstförderungsgesetz provides that the Federal Minister can use advisory boards and jury to prepare and advise funding matters of individual art sectors; there are currently 13 advisory boards and 50 jury boards. They have a consultative function. Members shall have the task of making recommendations on the content of the applications for funding of some 6 000 applications per year. This amendment complements this Art Promotion Act by a regulation which gives the Federal Chancellor the opportunity to set the meeting fees for the members of Advisory Boards and Jurys in the field of art promotion. Travel and cash payment can also be standardised by regulation. In 2014, 107 men and 123 women were active as experts in these advisory and jury committees. Around €187 000 was spent on travel expenses and meeting fees. If you divide that, you'll see that it's a reasonable size in this important matter. In the future, travel expenses will be reimbursed in accordance with the rules governing travel fees in the Federal Republic of Germany in 1955. The meeting fees shall be determined by the Registrar's regulation in accordance with the individual advisory and jury-specific requirements. In addition, in the interests of the administrative economy, the Regulation may also provide for a package. The new regulation, ladies and gentlemen, does not create any additional costs for the federal government, nor does it provide for any extension of these remunerations. That is, I think, a positive development towards economical administration and transparency. I would also like to maintain that the system, according to which independent experts in various councils and jury give their recommendations for the decision of the institute, has proved itself well over the years. These are experts, these people. are of the subject, they have high expertise and can safely assess proven structures in the field of contemporary art. At the same time, new developments and innovative projects in the field of art can be quickly responded to so that new impulses can be given sufficient space, because ultimately it is about creating positive framework conditions for art and providing optimal support for artists in their work. At this point I thank all the advisory and jury members for their valuable and supportive work. I would like to say to Mr Mölzer that this is in principle understandable and I can understand it. However, I would like to say - you mentioned it briefly - that: The real experts in Austria are not so broad. We believe that it has failed that we have used Austrian instead of foreign experts. It is essential, however, that if an expert is affected by his own project, she should not vote in this advisory board at all. I think that is a good solution, and that is why we would like to remain that way. Thank you.

Equally reluctant was the mandate of the holding itself to evaluate its own optimization potential, and the study of Bock and Kijk has found twice as high a savings potential. Why the Ministry has not questioned the difference in this potential for savings remains a mystery. It is perfectly incomprehensible, however, that the outcome of the Court of Auditors Subcommittee was not expected before the Federal Theatre Organisation Act was sent into the review. But apart from all these criticisms, in my view it is undisputed and a high cultural value that the Burgtheater is simply the best German-speaking theatre. It has produced a great tradition, great artists and audience lovers. We are very proud of it. That is why we need to take good care of it. - Thank you.

The Bundestheater therefore had around €200 million less of public funding available in these 14 years, so numerous savings were necessary, and they were also implemented. Essential savings were made in the personnel sector. The staff representatives reported to the Committee on the contribution of employees to saving. ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00086_d7e16324	Mr President. Minister! High house! In July, his "Working Group on the Reform of the Digital Media Market" was convened. It is likely to be a men's circle with representatives of the ORF, the VÖZ, a few politicians and other participants, for whom one does not necessarily have to gender. It is about a “education” through which “the domestic media diversity in times of global competition is to be sustained”. But the opposite happens. In Austria, we have in the media sector a political supply of ads in an order of 200 million € for the printing sector. There is an ORF entered into the committees, which is still subject to further restrictions by a disability to new technologies such as social media, app ban et cetera. That is, media policy in Austria is essentially power policy. Nor does the government do much more than cut down the already micky press promotion and fantasize about a right to benefit. [[Congratulation with NEOS and Greens.]] That is, this round of stakeholders and government parties is discussing the future of the media, aggregation, vertical integration, disintegration and over-the-top content. I don't think so. There's a lot of doubt about the fact that something's happening. In this case, it is not really a matter of balancing interests. In this case, it should be a paradigm change in media policy – we need this paradigm change. Current media policy does not remedy the traditional distortion that exists in the Austrian media market – which you have approved over the past decades – nor does it take into account the new challenges that digitalisation, networking and globalisation bring. What is the task of media policy in a democracy? – This is the addressing of market failures. 50, 60, 70 years ago, in a certain area, you had a market failure where it was necessary to build infrastructure where it was democratically legitimate to invest in it. There is no market failure in the infrastructure these days. There is no market failure in the distribution of content. There is no market failure in the technical production of content. At most there is still a market failure in the production of public-law content, in the creation of public value. This is exactly where media promotion has to be promoted in the 21st century. ==References== But you certainly don't use your media policy there. This also applies not only to the ORF, but to all media. How can you achieve this? – In two steps: First of all, media policy cannot be understood as a policy of power. This means the depoliticization of the ORF, the complete removal of direct media promotion, the removal of advertising, a structural reform in the ORF, a reduction of the Stiftungsrats analogous to a Supervisory Board of the AG, as we have already called for several times, a conversion of the Public Council to a real funder assembly and such small things as the removal of the hearing law of the national leaders. The second step would then be to promote diversity and journalism and a way from financing a media house to promoting public-law content. The whole thing is a process. After depoliticization of the ORF, one should talk about how to disintegrate this company vertically on the value chain. Distribution is, as has been said, something that the market can do today excellently. In other words, the concentration of support should cover the area where public-law content is produced. In addition, a new media promotion is needed, which does not promote distribution and organization, but instead promotes public value, technology and media. I expect these developments to be discussed in the National Council and not in master circles of stakeholders. I have brought along a motion for a resolution on opt-out from the GIS. It is also to be considered whether you should not evaluate this. I would therefore like to make the following request:

In fact, it is increasingly common for ORF moderators or those participating in the content design of consignments to be invited to participate in events, to appear there and obviously to have secondary jobs that may be well paid or that are paid in other forms by physical or indirect services. We'd be very interested in that. If one assumes an objectivity of a medium – that is always the topic of the ORF – and if one assumes that the medium is financed by means of fees, then we also want to know where these moderators and these employees add money and then whom they may be grateful or dependent on. I therefore make an appropriate request to ensure that: [[Quoted print version removed]] [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. Brosz.]] This would be an essential contribution to transparency. That's where we knew: Whom I eat, the song I sing. We would then have a concrete indication and could make a better picture of how transparent the ORF is and how independent its employees are.

It is only good if you use these costs in a synergies and not everyone has to build their own center. On the whole, the objective of this radio package is to strengthen the private sector and repair a wrong legal approach to the reduction ban.

Finally, we have to do what we liberals have been demanding for years, namely finally to hire more police officers.
